# Evidence ledger: Azure Verified Module adoption assessment

This is the evidence ledger for [the Azure Verified Module adoption assessment](avm-adoption-assessment.md). Every
`[L<n>]` marker on that page names one row here, and every row here is cited there at least once. It holds
624 rows and refers to 213 saved copies.

How to read a row:

- **A repository row** has a `path:line` source at commit `ce25ea4` (release 4.3.5). The quote is the entire line.
  "The project guidance file" is the guidance file at the repository root.
- **A URL row** was retrieved on 2026-11-13. The copy column gives the name of the saved response body and its SHA-256.
  The quote is copied from that saved body. For line-oriented files it is a whole line or sentence. For a JSON, CSV or
  HTML body, which has no sentence structure, it is an exact contiguous substring; an HTML quote keeps an inline tag or
  stops at one. A quote marked with a line range spans physical lines, each line break shown as one space.
- **A measurement row** has a source that starts with "measurement" or "probe log". It quotes a line of a log that the
  research wrote while running a command. The copy column names that log and its SHA-256.
- **An absence row** has a quote that starts with "n/a: absence". It holds the command that searched the saved copy
  and what it printed, because there is no text to quote.
- A pipe inside a quote is written `\|`. Nothing else in a quote is altered. A quote that itself holds a
  backtick is fenced with a longer run of backticks and one space of padding on each side.
- Rows L1 to L573 are in the order the page first cites them. Rows L574 to L624 were added after an
  independent audit of the page, in the order they were added.

The saved copies are third-party pages and local logs, so they are not committed. They are kept outside the repository
under the names this ledger uses. What a reader can check depends on the row:

- **A pinned URL** (a release tag or a commit) can be downloaded again, and the SHA-256 confirms that the body is the
  same one. The documentation and index files that were read from a default branch are cited at the commit that was its
  head on the check date; the body at that commit has the SHA-256 recorded here.
- **A URL without a pin**, such as a registry API response or a rendered documentation page, can have changed since the
  check date, so its SHA-256 may no longer match a fresh download.
- **A measurement log is not published, and a reader cannot obtain it.** Its SHA-256 only ties the row to the file the
  research kept. To check such a row, run the command its source names again and compare the quoted line. The two
  toolchains and the inputs are described on the assessment page.
